    47	/**
    48	 * Student-side: returns visible, unlocked classroom_items for the signed-in
    49	 * user. Keep the explicit student_id filter even though RLS also protects
    50	 * student accounts: admins can read every row, and may still open student-side
    51	 * routes while testing. Without this scope, an admin sees every student's work
    52	 * combined into one calendar.
    53	 */
    54	export function useClassroomItems() {
    55	  const { user } = useAuth();
    56	  return useQuery<ClassroomItem[]>({
    57	    queryKey: ['classroom-items', user?.id],
    58	    enabled: !!user,
    59	    staleTime: 60_000,
    60	    refetchOnWindowFocus: false,
    61	    queryFn: async () => {
    62	      const { data, error } = await supabase
    63	        .from('classroom_items')
    64	        .select('*')
    65	        .eq('student_id', user!.id)
    66	        .order('priority', { ascending: false })
    67	        .order('due_at', { ascending: true, nullsFirst: false })
    68	        .order('created_at', { ascending: false });
    69	      if (error) throw error;
    70	      return (data ?? []) as ClassroomItem[];
    71	    },
    72	  });
    73	}
    74	
    75	/**
    76	 * Admin-side: full read of a single student's items (regardless of
    77	 * visibility / unlock). Admin RLS bypass.
    78	 */
    79	export function useStudentClassroomItems(studentId: string | null) {
    80	  return useQuery<ClassroomItem[]>({
    81	    queryKey: ['classroom-items-admin', studentId],
    82	    enabled: !!studentId,
    83	    queryFn: async () => {
    84	      const { data, error } = await supabase
    85	        .from('classroom_items')
    86	        .select('*')
    87	        .eq('student_id', studentId)
    88	        .order('created_at', { ascending: false });
    89	      if (error) throw error;
    90	      return (data ?? []) as ClassroomItem[];
    91	    },
    92	  });
    93	}

35-  /**
36-   * The wrapper is a parser fix, not decoration. DOMPurify parses into a whole
37-   * document and returns only the body, and the HTML parser hoists a <style>
38-   * that OPENS the document into <head> — so a note whose design block comes
39-   * first would lose the entire design and render unstyled. Verified in Chrome
40-   * against DOMPurify 3.4.1: leading <style> is dropped, nested <style> survives
41-   * with @import, @media, @keyframes and custom properties intact. Parsing
42-   * inside a wrapper keeps the note in body flow; the wrapper is unwrapped below
43-   * so the rendered markup is exactly what was authored.
44-   */
45-  const sanitized = DOMPurify.sanitize(`<div ${NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR}="">${html}</div>`, {
46-    ADD_ATTR: ["loading", "sizes", "srcset", NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR],
47-    ADD_TAGS: ["style"],
48-    FORBID_TAGS: ["script"],
49-  });
50-  if (typeof document === "undefined") {
51-    return sanitized
52-      .replace(new RegExp(`^<div ${NOTE_WRAPPER_ATTR}="">`), "")

54-  }
55-  const template = document.createElement("template");
56-  template.innerHTML = sanitized;

     3	
     4	/**
     5	 * The single markdown renderer for every piece of RC content: passage text,
     6	 * summaries, prompts, choice text, and explanation bodies.
     7	 *
     8	 * Deliberately narrow: paragraphs, bold, italics, line breaks, blockquotes and
     9	 * lists render; links, images, headings and raw HTML do not. Lists are allowed
    10	 * because authored explanation blocks genuinely contain them, and dropping them
    11	 * would mangle the author's content.
    12	 */
    13	
    14	const components: Components = {
    15	  a: ({ children }) => <>{children}</>,
    16	  img: () => null,
    17	  // Authored content occasionally uses a heading where a bold line is meant;
    18	  // render it as an ordinary paragraph rather than injecting document structure.
    19	  h1: ({ children }) => <p>{children}</p>,
    20	  h2: ({ children }) => <p>{children}</p>,
    21	  h3: ({ children }) => <p>{children}</p>,
    22	  h4: ({ children }) => <p>{children}</p>,

    34	  return (
    35	    <div className={className}>
    36	      <ReactMarkdown components={components} skipHtml>
    37	        {children}
    38	      </ReactMarkdown>
    39	    </div>
    40	  );

=== CONTEXT.md ===
# Classroom

The student's own room: the first surface a 1:1 student lands on. It is a reading surface, not a workspace. Its language is second person, and the coach is never named in the furniture.

## Terms

**Classroom frame** — the persistent layout every Classroom route is drawn in: the cream left rail, an optional middle list, and the white reading surface. Implemented by `ClassroomLayout`. Navigating between surfaces it draws swaps the content only and never remounts the rail. A page "sits in the Classroom frame" regardless of its URL; `/flagged` and `/journal` both do.

**StudentShell** — the older, separate frame with its own 240px rail, still drawing `/mainhub`, `/practice` and the rest. A student moving between a Classroom-frame page and a StudentShell page sees the whole page rebuild. Reducing that boundary is ongoing work, one surface at a time.

**Reading column** — the white content column. Most routes use one reading column on the right. A Wrong answers detail uses a four-column Classroom frame: cream rail, entries list, vertical question, and review panel. Below its journal-only breakpoints the entries list becomes an overlay and the review panel moves under the question.

**Drill landing workspace** — the pre-question surface at `/classroom/drill`, drawn inside the Classroom frame. It includes drill selection, launch controls, practice insights, and today's work; it excludes the official question-answering experience.
_Avoid_: Drill page (when the question experience could be confused with the landing surface), Mainhub

**Question finder** — the one control that turns something a student can say about a question into that question opened. It takes free text, matched against question text, and it takes a question address (PT 90, Section 1, Question 12) typed or picked from PrepTest / Section / Question selectors. It searches both banks and groups its results by bank, Logical Reasoning first, because a hit lands differently in each: an LR hit opens that one question, an RC hit opens its whole passage with every question on it. The finder is a finding aid, not the scope of the work it opens. It appears on Classroom Home and in the Drill landing workspace; it never builds a set, it hands that to the Drill console.
_Avoid_: search bar, command palette, spotlight, quick access, find bar

**Drill console** — the control surface inside the Drill landing workspace where a student assembles a set: the filters, the preview of the exact set, and the...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

174 .impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md
# Surface brief: the classroom (`.classroom-root`)

**Scope.** Everything behind the `.classroom-root` wrapper: the rail, the section list,
the stage, and the routes under `/classroom`, `/flagged` and `/journal` that render
inside that shell. Not the bootcamps or the Activity Centre worlds, which have their own
wrappers.

**Register.** Product. Design serves the work; the student is here to read what their
coach wrote and to find the next thing to do.

**Status.** Sanctioned world, added to DESIGN.md's scoped-world list on 2026-09-08 by
Joshua's decision. The audit found the classroom running on its own typeface and a warm
palette while the app-wide record described the Workroom as neutral and Lexend-only. The
decision was that the code was right and the record was stale. This file is the record
catching up.

## What makes it its own world

**A warm paper ground, not the Workroom's neutral.** `--classroom-page` is `#ffffff`
with `--classroom-line` at `#f3e3d6` and `--classroom-hover` a warm translucent cream.
The rail reads as a study rather than an admin panel, and both independent design
assessments in the 2026-09-08 audit named that warmth as a genuine asset rather than
drift.

**Outfit, not Lexend.** `classroom.css` sets `font-family: "Outfit"` on the wrapper. The
app-wide Workroom face is Lexend, and Lexend renders nowhere inside the classroom. That
is deliberate as of this brief. Outfit is also the question lock, so LSAT text and the
room it sits in share a face, and a passage does not change shape when the student moves
between reading a note and reading a stimulus.

**IBM Plex Mono for instrument labels.** The rail group labels, the chevrons, the brand
lockup and the unread badge. Swapped from Roboto Mono on 2026-09-08: Roboto Mono was
undeclared anywhere in the design record, and IBM Plex Mono is the mono this project
already documents and already loads.

## Tokens this world owns

| Token | Light | Dark | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--classroom-page` | `#ffffff` | `#0f0e17` | the ground |
| `--classroom-surface` | `#ffffff` | `#0a0913` | panel and card ground |
| `--classroom-ink` | `#43403c` | `#fffffe` | primary text |
| `--classroom-ink-body` | `#43403c` | `#e5e5e5` | body copy |
| `--classroom-meta-text` | `#6d6a67` | `#a7a9be` | **any text that is muted** |
| `--classroom-meta-soft` | `rgba(67,64,60,.4)` | `#a7a9be` | **fills and borders only, never text** |
| `--classroom-line` | `#f3e3d6` | `#17161c` | hairline separation |
| `--classroom-line-strong` | `#9c8f85` | `#646176` | a border that is itself the affordance (WCAG 1.4.11) |
| `--classroom-line-on-tint` | `#8a7d72` | `#7a7789` | the same job, for a control whose ground is a tint rather than the page |
| `--classroom-accent` | `#a25704` | `#ff8906` | a fill that carries text: primary button, unread badge, unread dot |
| `--classroom-accent-ink` | `#ffffff` | `#0f0e17` | the label on that fill |
| `--classroom-link` | `#ff8906` | `#ff8906` | **decorative fills only** |
| `--classroom-link-text` | `#a25704` | `#ff8906` | orange that carries words |

## Named rules

**The text/fill split is the load-bearing rule here.** `--classroom-meta` and
`--classroom-meta-soft` are tuned as fills and measure 1.85:1 and 2.10:1 as text on the
page. Every muted *word* resolves from `--classroom-meta-text`. The same split governs
`--classroom-link` and `--classroom-link-text`. Getting this wrong put every date, every
rail group label and the unread badge below the AA floor for months.

**Opacity multiplies against the colour.** Swapping a token is not enough if an
`opacity` sits on the same rule. `.classroom-nav-group > b` was `#6d6a67` at `0.7` and
still measured 2.92:1. Muted text carries its colour in the token, not in an alpha.

**A themed pair, not a shared colour, for anything on an accent fill.** The amber that
works on the light ground is `#a25704`; on the dark ground it is `#ff8906` with near-black
on top. Reusing one value a...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

export async function publishSessionBundle(args: PublishArgs): Promise<PublishResult> {
  const { admin, studentId, draftId, bundle } = args;
  const targets = (args.only ?? unpublishedApprovedParts(bundle)).filter((k) =>
    unpublishedApprovedParts(bundle).includes(k),
  );

  if (targets.length === 0) {
    return { ok: true, published: [], bundle, nothing_to_publish: true };
  }

  // Work on a mutable copy so we can write posted flags back atomically.
  const next: SessionBundle = { ...bundle, parts: { ...bundle.parts } };
  const outcomes: PublishPartOutcome[] = [];

  for (const key of targets) {
    const part = next.parts[key];
    if (!part) continue;
    try {
      const title = (part.title?.trim() || PART_LABELS[key]).slice(0, 200);
      const markdown = partToMarkdown(key, part);

      // An approved part can still render to nothing (body edited blank in
      // review, or items emptied). Posting it would notify the student about a
      // blank recap, so fail this part closed and leave it unposted.
      if (!markdown) {
        outcomes.push({
          part: key,
          ok: false,
          error: `${PART_LABELS[key]} is empty — add content or exclude the part before publishing.`,
        });
        continue;
      }

      if (key === 'session_note') {
        const item = await createClassroomItem({
          studentId,
          createdBy: admin.id,
          type: 'session_recap',
          title,
          contentMarkdown: markdown,
          sourceTable: 'ai_ta_drafts',
          sourceId: draftId,
          status: 'assigned',
          visibleToStudent: true,
          metadata: { bundle_part: key },
        });
        const notify = await notifyStudentOfSessionRecap({
          admin,
          studentId,
          route: itemRoute(item.id),
        });
        outcomes.push({
          part: key,
          ok: true,
          classroom_item_id: item.id,
          notify_summary: notify.inbox_ok && notify.bell_ok
            ? 'Posted and notified'
            : 'Posted, partial notification',
        });
        next.parts[key] = { ...part, posted: true, classroom_item_id: item.id };
      } else {
        // homework + quiz: real assignable items; reuse the assignment notifier.
        const item = await createClassroomItem({
          studentId,
          createdBy: admin.id,
          type: 'homework',
          title,
          contentMarkdown: markdown,
          sourceTable: 'ai_ta_drafts',
          sourceId: draftId,
          status: 'assigned',
          visibleToStudent: true,
          metadata: { bundle_part: key },
        });
        const notify = await notifyStudentOfClassroomItem({
          adminUserId: admin.id,
          studentId,
          title,
          route: itemRoute(item.id),
          description: key === 'quiz' ? 'A short recall check from your session.' : undefined,
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
 * route resolver and the item view assumed every `homework` row was a mirror of
 * something else: the resolver sent it to `/classroom` and the item view
 * redirected it off its own page, so the button did nothing at all and the
 * direct link bounced back to Home.
 */
export function isSelfContainedItem(item: ClassroomItem): boolean {
  return !item.source_id && !!(item.content_html || item.content_markdown);
}

/**
 * Resolve a classroom_items row to the right student-facing route.
 * Returns the path to navigate to, or null if the item is not actionable.
 */
export function resolveItemRoute(item: ClassroomItem): string | null {
  switch (item.type) {
    case 'drill':
      if (item.source_table === 'guided_drill_assignments' && item.source_id) {
        return `/guided/${encodeURIComponent(item.source_id)}?returnTo=${encodeURIComponent('/classroom')}`;
      }
      if (item.source_id) return `/drill?assignmentId=${encodeURIComponent(item.source_id)}`;
      return '/drill';
    case 'homework':
      if (item.source_id) return `/classroom/${encodeURIComponent(item.source_id)}`;
      if (isSelfContainedItem(item)) return `/classroom/item/${encodeURIComponent(item.id)}`;
      return '/classroom';
    case 'activity':
      // source_id is the activity_assignments row id; source_table='activity_assignments'.
      if (item.source_id) return `/activities/${encodeURIComponent(item.source_id)}`;
      return '/activities';
    case 'bootcamp_module':
      if (item.source_id) return item.source_id; // expected to be a /bootcamp/* path
      return '/bootcamps';
    case 'session_recap':
    case 'smartboard_recap':
    case 'announcement':
    case 'note':
      return `/classroom/item/${encodeURIComponent(item.id)}`;
    default:
      return `/classroom/item/${encodeURIComponent(item.id)}`;
  }
}

description: Create ClassroomMarkdown renderer
command: cat > src/components/classroom/ClassroomMarkdown.tsx <<'EOF'
import * as React from "react";
import ReactMarkdown from "react-markdown";
import type { Components } from "react-markdown";

/**
 * The one renderer for a note or recap body stored as markdown.
 *
 * Why this exists: `classroom_items.content_markdown` was rendered as
 * `<p>{item.content_markdown}</p>`. Markdown is newline-significant and
 * `.classroom-prose p` sets no `white-space`, so every recap collapsed into a
 * single run-on paragraph carrying its own syntax: a student read
 * `- [ ] Do PT 141 S2 - [ ] Review your misses` on one line, and
 * `**1. What is a sufficient assumption?**` with the asterisks showing.
 *
 * That body is not hand-typed prose that happens to contain punctuation. It is
 * real markdown by construction: `partToMarkdown` in src/lib/sessionBundle.ts
 * emits `- [ ] `, `**n. prompt**` and `_Answer:_` from the AI TA bundle, and the
 * field it reads is named `body_markdown`.
 *
 * Deliberately narrow, matching RcMarkdown's posture:
 *
 * - Headings demote. A note body sits under the page's own `<h1
 *   class="classroom-title">`, so an authored `#` would open a second document.
 *   h1-h2 render as h2, h3-h6 as h3 — the two levels `.classroom-prose` styles.
 * - Raw HTML is skipped. HTML notes travel the `content_html` path through
 *   `sanitizeClassroomHtml`, which is what allows an authored `<style>` block.
 *   Nothing should reach the DOM from here without passing that.
 * - Task items render as a checkbox glyph rather than the literal `[ ]`.
 *   GitHub task lists need remark-gfm, which this project does not carry; the
 *   bundle emits them regardless, so the marker is translated here instead of
 *   adding a dependency for one syntax.
 * - External links open in a new tab so a note cannot navigate the student out
 *   of the classroom. In-app paths stay in place.
 */

const TASK_ITEM = /^\[([ xX])\]\s+/;

/** A task item's text arrives as the li's first child on a tight list. */
function splitTaskItem(children: React.ReactNode) {
  const kids = React.Children.toArray(children);
  const first = kids[0];
  if (typeof first !== "string") return null;
  const match = TASK_ITEM.exec(first);
  if (!match) return null;
  return {
    done: match[1].toLowerCase() === "x",
    rest: [first.slice(match[0].length), ...kids.slice(1)],
  };
}

const components: Components = {
  h1: ({ children }) => <h2>{children}</h2>,
  h2: ({ children }) => <h2>{children}</h2>,
  h3: ({ children }) => <h3>{children}</h3>,
  h4: ({ children }) => <h3>{children}</h3>,
  h5: ({ children }) => <h3>{children}</h3>,
  h6: ({ children }) => <h3>{children}</h3>,
  img: () => null,
  a: ({ href, children }) => {
    const external = !!href && /^https?:\/\//i.test(href);
    return (
      <a
        href={href}
        {...(external ? { target: "_blank", rel: "noreferrer noopener" } : {})}
      >
        {children}
      </a>
    );
  },
  li: ({ children }) => {
    const task = splitTaskItem(children);
    if (!task) return <li>{children}</li>;
    return (
      <li className="classroom-prose-task" data-done={task.done ? "" : undefined}>
        <span aria-hidden="true" className="classroom-prose-task-box">
          {task.done ? "✓" : ""}
        </span>
        <span className="classroom-prose-task-text">
          <span className="sr-only">{task.done ? "Done: " : "To do: "}</span>
          {task.rest}
        </span>
      </li>
    );
  },
};

interface Props {
  children: string | null | undefined;
  /** The prose container class. Callers outside `.classroom-root` pass their own. */
  className?: string;
}

export function ClassroomMarkdown({ children, className = "classroom-prose" }: Props) {
  if (!children || !children.trim()) return null;
  return (
    <article className={className}>
      <ReactMarkdown components={components} skipHtml>
        {children}
      </ReactMarkdown>
    </article>
  );
}

export default ClassroomMarkdown;
EOF

command: python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/components/classroom/ClassroomRouteViews.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

old = '''/**
 * First ~55 words of a note, for the landing preview.
 *
 * Notes are authored HTML and may carry their own <style>, so the tags are
 * stripped through a detached element rather than a regex, and any <style> or
 * <script> subtree is dropped first so CSS text never leaks into the excerpt.
 */
function noteExcerpt(item: {
  description: string | null;
  content_markdown?: string | null;
  content_html: string | null;
}) {
  const source = item.description || item.content_markdown || item.content_html || "";
  if (!source) return "";
  let text = source;
  if (/[<>]/.test(source)) {
    const holder = document.createElement("div");
    holder.innerHTML = source;
    holder.querySelectorAll("style, script").forEach((node) => node.remove());
    text = holder.textContent ?? "";
  }
  const words = text.replace(/\\s+/g, " ").trim().split(" ").filter(Boolean);
  if (words.length === 0) return "";
  return words.length <= 55 ? words.join(" ") : `${words.slice(0, 55).join(" ")}…`;
}'''

new = '''/**
 * Markdown reduced to the words it renders, for a plain-text excerpt.
 *
 * The excerpt is the only place a recap body is shown without going through
 * ClassroomMarkdown, so the syntax has to come off here instead. Without this
 * the featured note on the landing surface opened with `## What we worked on`
 * or `- [ ] Redo PT 141 S2` — the exact leak the reading view was just fixed
 * for. Structural markers are dropped; the words they wrapped are kept.
 */
function stripMarkdown(source: string) {
  return source
    .replace(/```[\\s\\S]*?```/g, " ")
    .replace(/`([^`]*)`/g, "$1")
    .replace(/!\\[[^\\]]*\\]\\([^)]*\\)/g, " ")
    .replace(/\\[([^\\]]*)\\]\\([^)]*\\)/g, "$1")
    .replace(/^\\s{0,3}>\\s?/gm, "")
    .replace(/^\\s{0,3}#{1,6}\\s+/gm, "")
    .replace(/^\\s*([-*+]|\\d+\\.)\\s+(\\[[ xX]\\]\\s+)?/gm, "")
    .replace(/^\\s*([-*_])\\s*\\1\\s*\\1[-*_\\s]*$/gm, " ")
    .replace(/(\\*\\*|__)(.*?)\\1/g, "$2")
    .replace(/(?<![\\w*])([*_])(?!\\s)(.+?)(?<!\\s)\\1(?![\\w*])/g, "$2");
}

/**
 * First ~55 words of a note, for the landing preview.
 *
 * Notes are authored HTML and may carry their own <style>, so the tags are
 * stripped through a detached element rather than a regex, and any <style> or
 * <script> subtree is dropped first so CSS text never leaks into the excerpt.
 */
function noteExcerpt(item: {
  description: string | null;
  content_markdown?: string | null;
  content_html: string | null;
}) {
  const source = item.description || item.content_markdown || item.content_html || "";
  if (!source) return "";
  let text = source;
  if (/[<>]/.test(source)) {
    const holder = document.createElement("div");
    holder.innerHTML = source;
    holder.querySelectorAll("style, script").forEach((node) => node.remove());
    text = holder.textContent ?? "";
  } else {
    text = stripMarkdown(text);
  }
  const words = text.replace(/\\s+/g, " ").trim().split(" ").filter(Boolean);
  if (words.length === 0) return "";
  return words.length <= 55 ? words.join(" ") : `${words.slice(0, 55).join(" ")}…`;
}'''

assert old in s, "excerpt block not found"

print('ok')

export function nextActionFor(items: ClassroomItem[]): ClassroomItem | null {
  if (!items?.length) return null;
  const candidates = items.filter(
    (i) => i.status === 'assigned' || i.status === 'in_progress',
  );
  if (candidates.length === 0) return null;
  // Highest priority, then earliest due date, then earliest unlock.
  return [...candidates].sort((a, b) => {
    if (b.priority !== a.priority) return b.priority - a.priority;
    const adue = a.due_at ? new Date(a.due_at).getTime() : Infinity;
    const bdue = b.due_at ? new Date(b.due_at).getTime() : Infinity;
    if (adue !== bdue) return adue - bdue;
    const aun = a.unlock_at ? new Date(a.unlock_at).getTime() : 0;
    const bun = b.unlock_at ? new Date(b.unlock_at).getTime() : 0;
    return aun - bun;
  })[0];
}

import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react';
import { Loader2 } from 'lucide-react';
import { Button } from '@/components/ui/button';
import { Input } from '@/components/ui/input';
import { Textarea } from '@/components/ui/textarea';
import { Label } from '@/components/ui/label';
import { toast } from 'sonner';
import { useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { createClassroomItem } from '@/lib/classroomItems';
import ClassroomMarkdown from '@/components/classroom/ClassroomMarkdown';

interface Props {
  studentId: string;
  adminUserId: string;
  onClose: () => void;
}

export default function RecapComposer({ studentId, adminUserId, onClose }: Props) {
  const qc = useQueryClient();
  const [title, setTitle] = useState('');
  const [body, setBody] = useState('');
  const [preview, setPreview] = useState(false);
  const [submitting, setSubmitting] = useState(false);
  const titleRef = useRef<HTMLInputElement>(null);
  const dirty = title.trim().length > 0 || body.trim().length > 0;

  // The composer was a bare fixed overlay: no Escape, no focus move, nothing
  // naming it to a screen reader. Escape closes, but never out from under a
  // half-written recap — losing ten minutes of typing to a stray keypress is
  // worse than one confirm.
  useEffect(() => {
    titleRef.current?.focus();
  }, []);

  useEffect(() => {
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (e.key !== 'Escape') return;
      e.stopPropagation();
      if (submitting) return;
      if (dirty && !window.confirm('Discard this recap?')) return;
      onClose();
    };
    window.addEventListener('keydown', onKey, true);
    return () => window.removeEventListener('keydown', onKey, true);
  }, [dirty, onClose, submitting]);

  const submit = async () => {
    if (!title.trim() || !body.trim()) {
      toast.error('Title and body required');
      return;
    }
    setSubmitting(true);
    try {
      await createClassroomItem({
        studentId,
        createdBy: adminUserId,
        type: 'session_recap',
        title: title.trim(),
        contentMarkdown: body,
        visibleToStudent: true,
        status: 'assigned',
      });
      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['classroom-items-admin', studentId] });
      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['classroom-items'] });
      toast.success('Recap posted to Classroom');
      onClose();
    } catch (e: any) {
      toast.error(e?.message ?? 'Failed to post recap');
    } finally {
      setSubmitting(false);
    }
  };

  return (
    <div
      className="fixed inset-0 z-50 bg-black/60 backdrop-blur-sm flex items-end md:items-center justify-center p-4"
      role="dialog"
      aria-modal="true"
      aria-labelledby="recap-composer-title"
    >
      <div className="w-full max-w-lg rounded-xl bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-800 shadow-xl p-5 space-y-4">
        <div>
          <h3 id="recap-composer-title" className="text-base font-semibold">
            Post a session recap
          </h3>
          <p className="text-xs text-zinc-400 mt-0.5">
            Posts immediately to the student's Classroom under Notes and recaps.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div className="space-y-3">
          <div>
            <Label className="text-xs" htmlFor="recap-title">
              Title
            </Label>
            <Input
              id="recap-title"
              ref={titleRef}
              value={title}
              onChange={(e) => setTitle(e.target.value)}
              className="mt-1"
              placeholder="Today's session"
            />
          </div>
          <div>
            <div className="flex items-center justify-between">
              <Label className="text-xs" htmlFor="recap-body">
                Body
              </Label>
              {/* The body is stored as markdown and the classroom renders it as
                  markdown. Without a preview the only place to find out what a
                  heading or a bullet list actually looks like was the student's
                  own screen, after posting. */}
              <button
                type="button"
                onClick={() => setPreview((p) => !p)}
                className="text-xs text-zinc-400 underline underline-offset-2 hover:text-zinc-100"
                aria-pressed={preview}
              >
                {preview ? 'Back to writing' : 'Preview'}
              </button>
            </div>
            {preview ? (
              <div className="mt-1 min-h-[13rem] rounded-md border border-zinc-800 bg-zinc-900/40 px-3 py-2">
                {body.trim() ? (
                  <ClassroomMarkdown className="recap-preview-prose">
                    {body}
                  </ClassroomMarkdown>
                ) : (
                  <p className="text-sm text-zinc-500">Nothing written yet.</p>
                )}
              </div>
            ) : (
              <Textarea
                id="recap-body"
                value={body}
                onChange={(e) => setBody(e.target.value)}
                rows={10}
                className="mt-1 text-sm"
                placeholder={
                  'What we worked on, in markdown.\n\n## What we covered\n\n- Sufficient assumption\n- Negating the answer\n\n**Next:** redo PT 141 S2.'
                }
              />
            )}
            <p className="mt-1 text-[11px] text-zinc-500">
              Markdown: ## for a heading, - for a bullet, **bold**.
            </p>
          </div>
        </div>
        <div className="flex justify-end gap-2 pt-1">
          <Button
            variant="ghost"
            size="sm"
            onClick={() => {
              if (dirty && !window.confirm('Discard this recap?')) return;
              onClose();
            }}
            disabled={submitting}
          >
            Cancel
          </Button>
          <Button size="sm" onClick={submit} disabled={submitting}>
            {submitting && <Loader2 className="w-4 h-4 mr-2 animate-spin" />}
            Post recap
          </Button>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
EOF

 * screen for checking what went out showed markdown source while the classroom
 * showed something else entirely. Same renderer on both sides now, so a heading
 * that looks wrong here looks wrong there.
 */
function RecapRow({ recap }: { recap: ClassroomItem }) {
  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
  const body = recap.content_markdown ?? '';

  return (
    <li className="rounded-lg border border-zinc-800 bg-zinc-900/40">
      <div className="px-4 py-3">
        <div className="text-sm font-medium text-zinc-100">{recap.title}</div>
        <div className="text-[11px] text-zinc-500 mt-0.5">
          {format(new Date(recap.created_at), 'MMM d, yyyy')}
        </div>
        {body && (
          <div
            className={
              open
                ? 'mt-2 prose prose-sm prose-invert max-w-none prose-p:my-2 prose-ul:my-2'
                : 'mt-2 prose prose-sm prose-invert max-w-none prose-p:my-2 prose-ul:my-2 line-clamp-4'
            }
          >
            <ClassroomMarkdown className="">{body}</ClassroomMarkdown>
          </div>
        )}
        {body && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={() => setOpen((v) => !v)}
            className="mt-2 text-[11px] text-zinc-400 underline underline-offset-2 hover:text-zinc-100"
            aria-expanded={open}
          >
            {open ? 'Show less' : 'Read the whole thing'}
          </button>
        )}
      </div>
    </li>
  );
}
"""
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('ok')
PY

      )

      await remove.mutateAsync({
        id: item.id,
        student_id: item.student_id,
        file_path: item.file_path,
      });
      toast.success("Removed from context");

input: {"action":"javascript_exec","tabId":"tab-1","text":"const host = document.getElementById('probe-host');\nconst a = document.getElementById('probe-link');\nconst kill = document.createElement('style');\nkill.textContent = '#probe-host, #probe-host * { transition: none !important; }';\ndocument.head.appendChild(kill);\nconst sleep = ms => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));\nfunction lum(rgb){const [r,g,b]=rgb.match(/\\d+/g).map(Number).map(v=>{v/=255;return v<=0.03928?v/12.92:Math.pow((v+0.055)/1.055,2.4);});return 0.2126*r+0.7152*g+0.0722*b;}\nfunction ratio(x,y){const L1=lum(x),L2=lum(y);return +(((Math.max(L1,L2)+0.05)/(Math.min(L1,L2)+0.05)).toFixed(2));}\nfunction hexToRgb(h){h=h.replace('#','');return 'rgb('+parseInt(h.slice(0,2),16)+','+parseInt(h.slice(2,4),16)+','+parseInt(h.slice(4,6),16)+')';}\nawait sleep(200);\nconst transitionToken = getComputedStyle(host).getPropertyValue('--classroom-transition').trim();\nconst light = { link: getComputedStyle(a).color, page: hexToRgb(getComputedStyle(host).getPropertyValue('--classroom-page').trim()) };\nlight.ratio = ratio(light.link, light.page);\ndocument.documentElement.classList.add('dark');\nawait sleep(300);\nconst dark = { link: getComputedStyle(a).color, page: hexToRgb(getComputedStyle(host).getPropertyValue('--classroom-page').trim()) };\ndark.ratio = ratio(dark.link, dark.page);\ndocument.documentElement.classList.remove('dark');\nkill.remove();\n({ transitionToken, light, dark })"}

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
`.classroom-mobile-bar-trigger`, the sticky bar’s section name promoted to a button. The
bar is sticky where the trigger was not, it names the section where the trigger said only
"Open the list", and it puts the room control and the section control in one row instead
of stacking two identically styled boxes that a student could not tell apart. The
index/detail split is `activeSection && activeId` in React rather than
`:has(.classroom-stage-index)` in CSS: an index route keeps a plain label, because a
control that opens a copy of what is on screen is still the no-op this shell removed once
before.

**A control whose border is its only edge uses `--classroom-line-strong`.** The warm
hairline `--classroom-line` measures 1.25:1 against the page and `--classroom-trigger`
measures 1.03:1, so a button built from that pair has nothing at 3:1 telling a student it
is a button. PRODUCT.md makes 3:1 on the visual boundary of an interactive control a
binding floor. `--classroom-line-strong` exists for this and measures 3.14:1 light and
3.21:1 dark; before 2026-09-09 it appeared once in 5,101 lines. Every interactive control
in this stylesheet now uses it: the mobile menu button, both `.classroom-button` variants,
sign-out, the overlay close, the drawer search, the account panel’s inputs, select and
ghost button, the announcement button, the toggle track and its knob, the journal and
review choices,
the textarea, the review control, and the five orientation controls. A rule is a control
if it carries `cursor: pointer`, `resize`, or an input/select/textarea selector; a scan on
that test returns zero controls left on the hairline.

**`--classroom-line-strong` is measured against the page and the panel, so a control on a
tinted ground takes `--classroom-line-on-tint` instead.** The two figures above are the
ratios against `--classroom-page` and `--classroom-surface`. They do not carry over to a
control sitting on something else. The toggle knob is the one case: it sits on the track,
which is `--classroom-hover` composited to `rgb(251,247,243)` light and `rgb(33,31,35)`
dark, and `--classroom-line-strong` read 2.68:1 there while reading 3.14:1 six pixels
away on the panel. Note the direction reverses between themes - the tinted ground is
darker than the panel on light and lighter than it on dark, so the token is darker than
`--classroom-line-strong` on light and lighter on dark. Measured 2026-09-09 on the
production build: knob border 3.75:1 against the track in both themes, 3.99:1 light and
4.55:1 dark against the knob fill. Before adding a third border token, check whether the
control's ground is genuinely a new one; two is the whole set.

`--classroom-line` stays what it always was: hairline separation between things, not the
edge of a control. It still carries the cards, the panels, the callouts, the prose media,
`.classroom-choice` (an `<li>` in an `<ol>`, not a button), `.classroom-review-correct-answer`
(a read-only display box, split out of the rule it used to share with the live control),
`.classroom-orientation-list-row` and `.classroom-orientation-day` (both `<div>`s).

**A border-colour change is not a focus indicator.** Three fields shipped
`outline: none` with only a border tint underneath: `.classroom-textarea`,
`.classroom-orientation-input` and `.classroom-review-control`. PRODUCT.md asks for a
visible focus indicator on every interactive element, and a tint that is now the same
colour as the rest border is no indicator at all. All three draw
`outline: 2px solid var(--classroom-ink)` on `:focus-visible`, the pattern
`.classroom-panel-search` already documented. No `outline: none` remains in this file.

**A focus ring uses `--classroom-link-text`, not `--classroom-link`.** The decorative
orange `#ff8906` measures 2.38:1 on the light ground, under the 3:1 a focus indicator
owes. `--classroom-link-text` is the themed pair that passes: 5.38:1 on light, the same
`#ff8906` on the near-black dark ground. `.classroom-icon-button` an...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

[external_agent_tool_result]
function RecapRow({ recap }: { recap: ClassroomItem }) {
  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
  const body = recap.content_markdown ?? '';

  return (
    <li className="rounded-lg border border-zinc-800 bg-zinc-900/40">
      <div className="px-4 py-3">
        <div className="text-sm font-medium text-zinc-100">{recap.title}</div>
        <div className="text-[11px] text-zinc-500 mt-0.5">
          {format(new Date(recap.created_at), 'MMM d, yyyy')}
        </div>
        {body && (
          <div
            className={
              open
                ? 'mt-2 prose prose-sm prose-invert max-w-none prose-p:my-2 prose-ul:my-2'
                : 'mt-2 prose prose-sm prose-invert max-w-none prose-p:my-2 prose-ul:my-2 line-clamp-4'
            }
          >
            <ClassroomMarkdown className="">{body}</ClassroomMarkdown>
          </div>
        )}
        {body && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={() => setOpen((v) => !v)}
            className="mt-2 text-[11px] text-zinc-400 underline underline-offset-2 hover:text-zinc-100"
            aria-expanded={open}
          >
            {open ? 'Show less' : 'Read the whole thing'}
          </button>
        )}
      </div>
    </li>
  );
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit markdown renderer
command: git add src/components/classroom/ClassroomMarkdown.tsx src/components/classroom/ClassroomMarkdown.test.tsx src/components/classroom/ClassroomRouteViews.tsx && git commit -q -F - <<'MSG' && git log --oneline -1
A recap is markdown, so render it as markdown

content_markdown was printed as <p>{item.content_markdown}</p>. Markdown is
newline-significant and .classroom-prose p sets no white-space, so a recap
reached the student as one run-on paragraph carrying its own syntax:
"- [ ] Redo PT 141 S2 - [ ] Blind review" on a single line, and
"**1. What is a sufficient assumption?**" with the asterisks showing.

The bodies are markdown by construction, not prose that happens to contain
punctuation. partToMarkdown emits "- [ ] ", "**n. prompt**" and "_Answer:_"
from a field named body_markdown, and every AI TA session bundle goes through
it. react-markdown was already a dependency.

ClassroomMarkdown is the one renderer. Headings demote to the h2/h3 the
stylesheet styles, so an authored "#" cannot open a second document under the
page's own h1. Raw HTML is skipped, because that path belongs to
sanitizeClassroomHtml and its <style> allowance. Task markers render as a box
rather than the literal [ ], since the bundle emits them and remark-gfm is not
in this project. External links open in a new tab; in-app paths stay put.

The landing excerpt strips the same syntax rather than rendering it: it is the
one place a body is shown without going through the renderer, and it was
opening notes with "## What we covered".

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
MSG
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit CSS fixes
command: git add src/components/classroom/classroom.css && git commit -q -F - <<'MSG' && git log --oneline -1
A note's links and lists say what they are

Two things in .classroom-prose, both measured in the browser against the live
sheet.

The link resolved from --classroom-link, which this file already records as
2.38:1 on the page. That is the text/fill split that held every date below the
floor for months, still live in the one place a student reads what their coach
wrote. --classroom-link-text measures 5.38:1 light and 8.06:1 dark. The rule
also set text-decoration: none and moved the colour to --classroom-ink on
hover, so a link was identifiable by colour alone and stopped being
identifiable at all under the cursor. Underlined in both states now.

The list markers were gone. Tailwind's preflight sets
"ol, ul, menu { list-style: none }" app-wide and this stylesheet restored only
the indent, so every list in every note rendered as unmarked indented lines and
a numbered list lost its numbers. That is a change of meaning, not of style:
"1, 2, 3" became three lines that no longer said they were in order.
list-style-type computed "none" on a note's <ul>; disc, decimal and circle are
declared explicitly now.

Task items get the box the renderer's markup expects, on
--classroom-line-strong at 3.14:1 light and 3.21:1 dark.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
MSG
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit routing fix
command: git add src/lib/classroomItems.ts src/lib/classroomItems.route.test.ts && git commit -q -F - <<'MSG' && git log --oneline -1
The homework posted with a recap is reachable

publishSessionBundle stamps every part of a session bundle with
source_table 'ai_ta_drafts' and the draft id, including the homework part,
which is a homework row carrying its own markdown and no assignment row
anywhere. resolveItemRoute read the bare source_id and sent the student to
/classroom/<draft uuid>, where the assignment view looked for a
homework_assignments row that has never existed and rendered "Assignment not

source_id says what a row was made from, not that the thing it was made from

Stop posting recaps blind

Two gaps on the teacher side, both about not being able to see what the student
sees.

The composer had no preview. The body is stored as markdown and the classroom
renders it as markdown, so the only place to find out what a heading or a
bullet list looked like was the student's own screen, after posting. It is also
a real dialog now: role, aria-modal, an accessible name, focus into the title
field, and Escape that asks before discarding a half-written recap.

The dossier could post a recap and could not show one. TeacherRecaps was the
only screen that listed them and nothing routes to it, so once a recap went out
the only way to check it was to be the student. PostedRecapsList puts what has
been posted under the composer, rendered through the same ClassroomMarkdown the
classroom uses, so a heading that looks wrong here looks wrong there.

Not styled with "prose prose-sm prose-invert". @tailwindcss/typography is in
package.json but is not registered in tailwind.config.ts, so every prose* class
in this codebase emits nothing: zero .prose rules exist in the app's
stylesheets, and a rendered h2 computes to the inherited body colour at 16px.
Arbitrary variants are core Tailwind and actually emit. The four other surfaces
using prose classes are untouched and still unstyled.

A note can be read, and cannot be deleted by one click

The trash icon deleted a note or an uploaded transcript on a single click, with
no confirm and no undo, sitting one row away from every other row's identical
icon. A transcript is often the only copy of what was said in a session. It
asks now, naming what it is about to remove.

The pending flag was global: remove.isPending greyed out every trash icon in
both lists while one delete was in flight. It tracks the row instead.

And a note was two lines of preview and nothing else, so the rest of what was
written had no reader anywhere in the app. Rows that carry more than their
preview expand.

Six commits on the Notes and recaps surface, student side and teacher side. All measured or tested, not inferred.

## What was wrong

**A recap was printed, not rendered.** `content_markdown` went through `<p>{item.content_markdown}</p>`. Markdown is newline-significant and `.classroom-prose p` sets no `white-space`, so a recap arrived as one run-on paragraph carrying its own syntax. This is not a hypothetical: `partToMarkdown` emits `- [ ] `, `**n. prompt**` and `_Answer:_` from a field named `body_markdown`, and every AI TA session bundle publishes through it.

**Lists had no markers.** Tailwind's preflight sets `ol, ul, menu { list-style: none }` app-wide and `.classroom-prose` restored only the indent. A numbered list lost its numbers, which changes meaning.

**A link in a note measured 2.38:1** on `--classroom-link`, with `text-decoration: none` and a hover that moved it to the plain ink colour.

**The homework attached to a recap was unreachable.** `publishSessionBundle` stamps every bundle part with `source_table: 'ai_ta_drafts'`; `resolveItemRoute` read the bare `source_id` and sent the student to `/classroom/<draft uuid>`, where the assignment view rendered "Assignment not found".

**Recaps were posted blind.** No preview in the composer, and no list of what had already been posted on the live teacher surface — `TeacherRecaps` has no route.

**A note or transcript deleted on one click**, no confirm, no undo.

## Measured

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| note link, light | 2.38:1 | **5.38:1** |
| note link, dark | — | **8.06:1** |
| task box on page | — | 3.14:1 light / 3.21:1 dark |
| `<ul>` list-style-type | `none` | `disc` |

Taken in the browser against the served stylesheet with transitions disabled, both themes.

## Verified

- `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` — exit 0
- `npx eslint` on all changed files — exit 0
- `npx vitest run src/components/classroom src/lib/classroomItems*` — 14 files, 133 tests, exit 0 (8 new for the renderer, 3 new for the routing fix)
- `ClassroomMarkdown` and `RecapComposer` mounted from source in a real browser against the real stylesheet; markup, computed styles and both themes checked

Not verified: nothing behind a login. This session could not authenticate, so `/classroom/recaps` and the dossier were not walked end to end as a signed-in user.

## Not done, deliberately

- `@tailwindcss/typography` is a dependency but is **not registered in `tailwind.config.ts`**, so every `prose*` class in this repo emits nothing — zero `.prose` rules exist in the app's stylesheets. Four other surfaces rely on those classes (`OperatorChat`, `DraftReplyCard`, `DeskChatPanel`, `TutorCorpus`) and are still unstyled. This PR sidesteps it rather than registering the plugin, which would change all four at once.
- Three files on this surface have no importers: `src/pages/ClassroomRecaps.tsx`, `src/components/classroom/RecapsSection.tsx`, `src/pages/ClassroomItem.tsx`. Left on disk; the comment that cited one of them as a source of truth is corrected.
- Three remaining `--classroom-link` uses that carry words, all outside the reading column: `.classroom-message-link`, `.classroom-annotate-button-active`, `.classroom-orientation-eyebrow`. Named in the surface brief.

    screens: [
    {
      id: "ch1-anticipating-the-authors-purpose",
      type: "prose",
      title: "Anticipating the Author’s Purpose",
      body: "\n    <p>The first and perhaps most important thing to remember about approaching an RC passage is a core skill we have already learned when practicing Logical Reasoning Questions: We must be aware of the passage structurally.</p>\n    <p>Most LR stimuli consisted of arguments. In an argument, there will be a main conclusion and supporting premises. When approaching an LR question argument, we consistently ask ourselves, “what is the author’s main point?” or “what is the main conclusion of this argument?” This applied not only to Find the Conclusion Questions but to every question type from the Assumption Family (Flaw, SA, NA, Strengthen, Weaken, etc.) and some more. When reading an RC passage, that is precisely the same approach we should take. As we go through the passage, we are constantly asking ourselves, <strong>“what is the main point of this passage? What is the author trying to convey, and what kind of supporting information are they using?”</strong></p>\n    <p>But then again, RC passages are much longer than the arguments we see in LR stimuli. As a result, many of us end up mindlessly reading/browsing through the information without really thinking about what the author is trying to say. We end up finishing the passage but can’t remember anything. The bits and pieces of information we recall are fragmented and piecemeal. When we finally get to the questions, they stress us out, and we have no idea what to do.</p>\n    <p>Because time is so limited in RC, we don’t have the luxury of wasting any of it. We must engage with the passage from the beginning and practice <strong>active reading</strong>. Rather than mindlessly wandering through the passage, we need to ask ourselves questions, find the answers in the passages, and reorganize the information we just read structurally.</p>\n    <p>Let me show you how:</p>",
    }
    ],
  },
  {
    name: "Your first read",
    screens: [
    {
      id: "ch2-start-with-the-purpose",
      type: "prose",
      title: "Start with the purpose",
      body: "<p>We start by anticipating the author’s purpose. In other words, as soon as we begin reading a passage, we ask ourselves, “why is the author writing this passage?” Just like how each LR argument will contain a main conclusion, and all that information will make sense once you have isolated what the conclusion is; if we can figure out the main point/conclusion of a passage, then reading and understanding the passage will become so much easier.</p>",
    },

import io, os
fixed=[]

    dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d != 'node_modules' and not d.startswith('.')]
    for name in files:
        if not name.endswith(('.mjs','.cjs','.js')): continue
        p=os.path.join(root,name)

"

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit the typography plugin registration
command: git add -A .gitattributes tailwind.config.ts && git commit -q -F - <<'MSG' && git log --oneline -1
Register the typography plugin, on this app's tokens

@tailwindcss/typography was a dependency that was never registered. Measured
2026-09-09: zero .prose rules in the served stylesheets, and a rendered <h2>
inside .prose computing to the inherited body colour at 16px, the same as a
paragraph. Thirteen live surfaces were styling markdown with classes that did
not exist.

Registering it alone would have made several of them worse. The app is dark at
:root and flips to light with a .light class, so darkMode: ["class"] means the
dark: variant almost never fires, and the four call sites written as
dark:prose-invert would have landed on Tailwind's own gray-700 body text
against a near-black ground.

So both the DEFAULT and invert keys resolve from --foreground,
--muted-foreground, --border and --muted. Prose is then correct in either theme
whether or not a call site asks for prose-invert, and dark: never has to fire
for text to be readable. Measured on the production build in both themes:
18.73:1 light, 17.95:1 dark, h2 at 20px/700, disc and decimal markers, links
underlined at a 2px offset.

.classroom-prose is untouched. It is the classroom's own stylesheet and stays
hand-written.

The .gitattributes is unrelated to the above and is explained in its own
comment: a shebanged .mjs under scripts/ with CRLF line endings cannot be
imported by a vitest test.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
MSG
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Commit the line-ending fix and guard
command: git add .gitattributes src/lib/__tests__/lineEndings.test.ts && git commit -q -F - <<'MSG' && git log --oneline -1
A shebang and CRLF together made a test suite disappear

src/lib/__tests__/questionStatsBuilder.test.ts failed to load on main with
"SyntaxError: Invalid or unexpected token" reported against its own line 4 — a
comment. All 17 of its tests never ran.

The previous investigation cleared that file and was right to. It was
byte-identical to origin/main, it was valid UTF-8, and node --check passed on
both of its imports. The problem was in
scripts/knowledge/build-question-stats.mjs, which the reported position never
pointed at.

Isolated in a clean checkout, one variable at a time. CRLF on the test file
alone: passes. CRLF on both imports: fails. CRLF on build-question-stats.mjs
alone: fails. That same CRLF file with its #!/usr/bin/env node removed: passes.
Both halves are needed, which is why the 1,796 CRLF files under src/ are
harmless — nothing strips a shebang from them.

.gitattributes pins scripts/** to LF. core.autocrlf is already 'input' here so
git writes LF on checkout; what this adds is that a tool which rewrites a whole
file cannot quietly leave CRLF behind. git status will not catch it either,
because normalisation on add makes a CRLF working copy read as clean — so the
test is the part that speaks up, and it reads the files rather than importing
them so it still runs when the thing it checks is what is broken.

The 38 shebanged scripts that were CRLF in the working copy are now LF. Their
contents are unchanged; git records no diff for them.

[/external_agent_tool_call]

## Round two: plugin registered, orphans deleted, the vanished test suite explained

### The typography plugin is registered — and the palette is this app's tokens

I under-reported the scale in the original description: it was **13 live surfaces**, not four, styling markdown with classes that emitted nothing.

Registering it alone would have made several of them **worse**. The app is dark at `:root` and flips to light with a `.light` class, so `darkMode: ["class"]` means the `dark:` variant almost never fires — the call sites written `dark:prose-invert` would have landed on Tailwind's own gray-700 body text against a near-black ground.

So both the `DEFAULT` and `invert` keys resolve from `--foreground`, `--muted-foreground`, `--border`, `--muted`. Prose is correct in either theme whether or not a call site asks for `prose-invert`.

Measured on the **production build**, both themes, transitions disabled:

| | light | dark |
|---|---|---|
| body on ground | 18.73:1 | 17.95:1 |
| h2 | 20px / 700 | 20px / 700 |
| list markers | disc / decimal | disc / decimal |
| links | underlined, 2px offset | underlined, 2px offset |

`.classroom-prose` is untouched — the classroom's own stylesheet stays hand-written.

### The four orphans are deleted

`pages/ClassroomRecaps.tsx`, `RecapsSection.tsx`, `pages/ClassroomItem.tsx`, `teacher/TeacherRecaps.tsx`. One of them, `pages/ClassroomItem.tsx`, carried a `whitespace-pre-wrap` on the markdown body that the live view did not — the dead copy rendered a recap *better* than the one students used.

`src/pages/ClassroomTAAssignmentDetail.tsx` is a fifth orphan of the same kind. Left in place: it belongs to the assignment surface, not this one, and nobody has been asked about it.

### `questionStatsBuilder.test.ts` — it was never that file

It failed with `SyntaxError: Invalid or unexpected token` at **its own line 4, a comment**. All 17 tests never ran. The previous investigation cleared the file and was right to — byte-identical to origin/main, valid UTF-8, `node --check` passing on both imports.

The problem was `scripts/knowledge/build-question-stats.mjs`. Isolated one variable at a time in a clean checkout:

| | result |
|---|---|
| CRLF on the test file alone | passes |
| CRLF on both imports | fails |
| CRLF on `build-question-stats.mjs` alone | **fails** |
| same file, same CRLF, `#!/usr/bin/env node` removed | **passes** |

A shebang **and** CRLF together. That is why the 1,796 CRLF files under `src/` are harmless — nothing strips a shebang from them.

`.gitattributes` pins `scripts/**` to LF. `git status` would never have caught it: `core.autocrlf=input` normalises on add, so a CRLF working copy reads as clean. `src/lib/__tests__/lineEndings.test.ts` is the guard — it *reads* the files rather than importing them, so it still runs when the thing it checks is what is broken. The 38 shebanged scripts that were CRLF in the working copy are now LF; git records no diff for them.

### Verified as the signed-in user

Joshua opened the build in his own browser mid-session, which closed the gap the original description flagged:

- `/classroom/recaps` renders on his real account — 3 notes, correct grouping, no markdown syntax in the featured excerpt
- opening a real authored-HTML note: the new `list-style-type` gives its four-move list proper bullets, and it reads as intended rather than as a regression
- the dossier's new **Posted to their Classroom** section, on a real student: 4 posted notes — and it immediately surfaced that **two of them are marked hidden from the student**, including a duplicate title. That is a data question for you, not a bug in this PR.
- that same check found the section showed a title and a date and nothing else, because every posted note is `content_html` rather than markdown. Fixed: the row falls back to a plain-text excerpt taken through a detached element so a note's own `<style>` cannot leak in.

### Test status

`tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · `eslint` clean on every changed file · full suite **4,398 passed of 4,405**.

The 5 remaining failures are in `BookingOfferHub.test.tsx` and `SingleSession.test.tsx`. They fail in isolation too, and neither imports anything this branch touches — those two files are unchanged from `origin/main`, so this run *is* the origin/main result for them. Pre-existing, and not investigated here.

# Session Handoff — Notes and recaps audit shipped and merged (PR #108), plus the typography plugin, five dead files, and the vanished test suite

## Where it started
Picked up a handoff on the classroom remediation with the instruction to audit and improve the Notes and recaps component overall, for students and for Joshua. The audit found the live reading view printed `content_markdown` as one `<p>`, so every AI-TA session recap reached students as a run-on line with its own markdown syntax showing. Joshua then said yes to all three follow-ups I raised (register `@tailwindcss/typography`, delete the dead files, fix `questionStatsBuilder.test.ts`), then "delete the fifth orphan too and merge it."

## Decisions locked + what shipped
All on `main` via [PR #108](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/108), merge commit `c9c60d9`, merged 2026-09-09 22:34 UTC. 11 commits, all path-scoped, none carrying another session's work.

- `ClassroomMarkdown` renderer — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\classroom\ClassroomMarkdown.tsx`. react-markdown (already a dep), headings demote to h2/h3, raw HTML skipped (that path is `sanitizeClassroomHtml`), `- [ ]` task items render as a box, external links open a new tab. Wired into `ClassroomItemView`. `noteExcerpt` strips markdown for the landing preview. 8 tests.
- `.classroom-prose a` → `--classroom-link-text` + underline. Measured 5.38:1 light / 8.06:1 dark (was 2.38:1, `text-decoration: none`, hover to plain ink).
- `.classroom-prose ul/ol` list markers restored — Tailwind preflight sets `list-style: none` app-wide and the stylesheet only restored the indent. Numbered lists had no numbers. Verified on Joshua's real "Conditional Logic cheat sheet" note that the new bullets read as intended.
- `resolveItemRoute` homework fix — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\lib\classroomItems.ts`. Bundle-published homework carries `source_table: 'ai_ta_drafts'`; it was routed to `/classroom/<draft uuid>` → "Assignment not found". Only `homework_assignments` is openable now; `ClassroomItemView` got its self-contained guard back. 3 tests.
- `RecapComposer` — live preview, `role="dialog"`, aria-modal, focus to title, Escape with discard-confirm. `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\teacher\RecapComposer.tsx`.
- `PostedRecapsList` — new "Posted to their Classroom" section in `DossierNotes`; renders markdown via the same renderer, falls back to a plain-text excerpt for `content_html` notes (found necessary on a real student: all 4 posted notes were HTML). `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\teacher\PostedRecapsList.tsx`.
- `TEACHER_MARKDOWN_CLASS` — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\teacher\teacherMarkdown.ts`. Explicit arbitrary-variant utilities; deliberately not `prose*`.
- `StudentNotes` — delete now confirms and names the item; pending is per-row; notes expand to full text.
- `@tailwindcss/typography` registered — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\tailwind.config.ts`. Both `DEFAULT` and `invert` keys resolve from `--foreground`/`--muted-foreground`/`--border`/`--muted`. Reason: app is dark at `:root`, light via `.light`, so `dark:prose-invert` never fires; Tailwind's gray palette would have been unreadable on dark. 13 surfaces used prose classes that emitted nothing. Measured on the production build: 18.73:1 light, 17.95:1 dark.
- Five orphans deleted — `pages/ClassroomRecaps.tsx`, `components/classroom/RecapsSection.tsx`, `pages/ClassroomItem.tsx`, `pages/teacher/TeacherRecaps.tsx`, `pages/ClassroomTAAssignmentDetail.tsx`. Zero importers each. Nothing under `/classroom` has a routed view and a dead twin now.
- `questionStatsBuilder.test.ts` root cause — a shebang AND CRLF together on `scripts/knowledge/build-question-stats.mjs`. Isolated one variable at a time; the error pointed at the test's line 4 (a comment), which is why the prior investigation cleared the test file. `.gitattributes` pins `scripts/**` to LF; `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\lib\__tests__\lineEndings.test.ts` guards it by reading, not importing. 38 shebanged scripts in the main checkout normalised to LF (content unchanged, git records no diff).
- Surface brief updated — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\.impeccable\surfaces\classroom.md`: five new named rules, corrected stale focus-ring open-case list, records the deletions and the plugin.
